We started this journey as best friends and our relationship will continue to be a priority not only for us but for our dog Charlie.”

Brittany and Tyler’s divorce is reportedly finalized five months after she filed.

I’m saying weirdly because it’s very easy for me to get in a relationship, and I’ve been actively trying to be alone and be with myself and just feel the feelings and not have to band-aid it with sparkly, shiny, emotional things.”

She adds that she’s been “in and out” of some flings, but has been pretty much keeping the vibes casual.

It was low-key and private.” Apparently Brittany’s friends “love Hunter for her” and say, “He treats her well. They are both very low-key people.”

#6847. Why Don’t You … Take Fashion Inspiration from the World’s Great Performers?

Diana Vreeland had a singular take on not only fashion but life. Her eccentricity has become folklore and was best exemplified in an illustrated column of irreverent advice for chic living she called “Why Don’t You?” Resurrected and reimagined for modern life, the column, now written by Bazaar’s executive digital director, Lynette Nylander, will dispense tips on how to live stylishly, decadently, and daringly.

Here’s a thought: Don’t get dressed. Mount a production. Put on a show. Go on tour. And if the reviews are lukewarm, don’t change the costume; blame the critic! For the performance issue, Lynette Nylander takes cues from the stage’s most stylish to arm you with tips for stepping out.

that the “effortless” look took 40 minutes. The “I just threw it on” leather jacket, the smudged eye makeup, the shirt that looks slept in: All of it is stagecraft. Miley Cyrus’s new rock-star look personifies this. She’s not someone to shy away from an “era,” and this one is all tousled hair, loads of lashes, and leather and lace from the likes of Tom Ford, Roberto Cavalli, and Saint Laurent. Effortless? No. Fabulous? Absolutely!

some noise. Taffeta rustles, silk whispers, embellishments jangle, and leather creaks with intent. Performance is not only visual. The swish of a skirt on a staircase is part of the entrance, and the weight of crepe hugging the hips is part of the walk. The late, great Tina Turner was a master of clothes that created a symphony as she writhed around the stage; often, they were the creations of the late, great Bob Mackie. Björk went viral earlier this year at the Venice Biennale in a neon-pink Bottega Veneta dress made of fiberglass shards that jingle-jangled together as she fist-pumped. The lesson? Dress for the ear as well as the eye.

the precision of a uniform. The chef’s whites, the lawyer’s suit, the professor’s tweeds, the tailored black of the gallery assistant: Each is a costume designed with a very particular audience in mind. Even people who wear the same thing every day are making a statement, and the strongest of them curate that repetition with real rigor. Consistency, done well, is a kind of theater. Just don’t make it boring! Consider Steve Jobs, who famously wore only Issey Miyake turtlenecks and Levi’s. Truly the only tech titan to ever have taste!

the art of quick change. For Céline Dion’s fall residency at the Plénitude Arena in Paris, underneath the spotlight wasn’t the only site where a performance took place. Like all true divas, Dion knows that every moment of every day is an opportunity to perform. Outside of the Royal Monceau hotel, her home in Paris, where her faithful fans lined up to greet her, she treated onlookers to a dance, a song, an air-kiss, and always a fashion show: off-the-runway looks from Jacquemus, Loewe, and Phoebe Philo. As they say, all the world’s a stage.

the ordinary garment its moment. Not every act needs sequins. A perfect white tee, a pair of jeans that have taken on the shape of a life, a forever leather jacket worn with confidence: these are the understudies who steal the show. Performance in fashion is not always about drama. Sometimes it is the quiet authority of a simple thing worn exactly right. A disciple of this way of dressing? Kim Gordon, whose effortless style, both onstage and offstage, has made her your favorite celebrity’s favorite celebrity for the past 40 years.

#6846. The Best Shearling Shoes for Cold-Weather Style and Comfort

Cozy, polished pairs to wear all winter long.

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

There’s a reason shearling keeps coming back every cold season. It’s the rare material that manages to be cozy and stylish at once, with a plush, cloud-like lining that wraps your feet in warmth and a rich texture that instantly makes an outfit feel more intentional. What began as practical gear for sheepherders and pilots has become a fixture of modern closets, showing up on everything from chic boots to sleek slippers, ballet flats, and fashion-forward sneakers.

Part of its staying power is function. Shearling is naturally insulating, breathable, and moisture-wicking, so your feet stay warm without overheating. Versatility is another reason why shearling has become so popular. The same material that elevates a pair of jeans and a sweater on a weekend errand run can make lounging at home feel like a small luxury.

Whether you’re hunting for the perfect slip-on to kick off at the door, a boot to carry you through winter, or a statement piece that turns heads, below are the must-have shearling shoes that earn a spot in your rotation this season.

Worn with tailored trousers, a slouchy knit, or bare ankles and a long wool coat, the shearling ballet flat moves effortlessly between the polished and the pared-back. The easiest way to make winter feel a little more cozy and luxurious.

Le Monde Beryl Apres Ski shearling-lined suede ballet flats

The loafer, softened for winter. A shearling lining lends quiet warmth to a classic silhouette, proving that heritage tailoring and true comfort can share the same shoe.

Slip them on with a slouchy trouser and a thick knit sock, or pair them with a midi skirt and a heavy coat. The shearling clog is the effortless finishing touch for cold-weather dressing.

Stuart Weitzman Benni Shearling-Lined Suede Mules

#6842. The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping’s Cast Is Totally Stacked

See Joseph Zada, Whitney Peak, Elle Fanning, Kieran Culkin, Jesse Plemons, and Glenn Close in the new movie

The Hunger Games franchise is welcoming a new installment, Sunrise on the Reaping, the sixth film adaptation of Suzanne Collins’s best-selling dystopian saga.

Releasing on November 20, the film boasts a star-studded cast of about 33 actors and counting, poised to bring a blend of familiar names and new faces to the world of Panem.

Ahead of the film, we’re reacquainting ourselves with beloved characters from the original trilogy, like Effie Trinket (Elle Fanning), Caesar Flickerman (Kieran Culkin), Beetee (Kelvin Harrison Jr.), and Wiress (Maya Hawke). Even the completely new characters, like Maysilee Donner and Lenore Dove, have family ties to well-known characters in the franchise.

Based on Collins’s second prequel novel, Sunrise on the Reaping delves into the brutal 50th Hunger Games, spotlighting a young Haymitch Abernathy 24 years before he became Katniss and Peeta’s mentor. We, as readers, thought we knew a detailed account of the second Quarter Quell from Mockingjay, but as we learn in the Sunrise on the Reaping novel, much of what we learned was manipulated by the Capitol to be used as propaganda.

This much is still true: With twice the tributes, the 50th Hunger Games doubles the bloodshed, trauma, and size of the cast. To prepare for the inevitably heart-wrenching film, get to know the extensive cast.

Joseph Zada is a 21-year-old Australian actor best known for playing Johnny Sinclair in the television series We Were Liars and Cal Trask in East of Eden.

Zada shows a very different side of Woody Harrelson’s character, before the biting sarcasm and heavy drinking. In the new film, we follow 16-year-old Haymitch’s harrowing journey through the 50th Hunger Games and, arguably, a more distressing journey after victory. We knew Zada was perfect for the role when he discussed re-creating Harrelson’s Mockingjay Part 2 red-carpet pajama moment.

Elle Fanning is a 28-year-old American actress best known for her roles as Aurora in Maleficent, Syvie Russo in A Complete Unknown, and Catherine in The Great, as well as being half of an iconic sister duo with Dakota Fanning.

While Trinket is only a side character in Sunrise on the Reaping, her casting was one of the most highly anticipated, with fans almost unanimously agreeing that Fanning was the only correct choice to reprise Elizabeth Banks’s character. In Sunrise on the Reaping, Trinket is a young member of the Capitol styling and beauty team for District 12.

It’s possible that Suzanne Collins got tired of the endless Coriolanus Snow thirst edits on TikTok and went ahead and cast Voldemort. No, really, 63-year-old English actor Ralph Fiennes is best known for his role as Voldemort in the Harry Potter film series.

Fiennes plays President Snow, a younger version of the late Donald Sutherland’s character in the original trilogy and an older version of Tom Blythe’s character in The Ballad of Songbirds and Snakes. President Snow reaches his peak level of viciousness in Sunrise on the Reaping, and Fiennes is the perfect vessel to carry out Snow’s evil plans.

Kieran Culkin is a 44-year-old actor best known for his roles in A Real Pain and Succession. Despite recently winning an Oscar, Emmy, Golden Globe, and SAG Award, his brother Macaulay still has him beat in household name recognition for his childhood role in Home Alone. Reprising Stanley Tucci's role as Hunger Games host Caesar Flickerman may change that.

Jesse Plemons is a 38-year-old actor best known for his roles in Friday Night Lights and Breaking Bad (and for being married to Kirsten Dunst). In another impeccable casting choice, Plemons also played the son of the original Plutarch (the late Philip Seymour Hoffman) in The Master (2012).

In both Sunrise on the Reaping and the original trilogy, Plutarch Heavensbee is an old-money Capitol citizen whose morally gray philosophies make him one of the franchise’s most fascinating characters. In the new film, we see him as a young, ambitious cameraman, years before he became head gamemaker.

Lili Taylor is a 59-year-old American actress and author best known for her roles in Mystic Pizza and The Conjuring. She has the perfect sweet smile to play Mags Flanagan, the victor of the 11th Hunger Games from District 4.

Mags, previously played by the late Lynn Cohen, was introduced in Catching Fire when she volunteered to take Annie Cresta's place in the 75th Hunger Games. In Sunrise on the Reaping, Mags acts as District 12's mentor, as they have no surviving winners of their own.

Maya Hawke is the 28-year-old daughter of Uma Thurman and Ethan Hawke, best known for her roles as Robin in Stranger Things and Anxiety in Inside Out 2.

She plays Wiress, the District 3 victor fresh out of the 49th Hunger Games. Amanda Plummer played the character in Catching Fire, when she was reaped with Beetee as the 75th Hunger Games District 3 tributes.

Kelvin Harrison Jr. is a 32-year-old actor who previously played the son of the original Beetee (Jeffrey Wright) in Monsters (2018).

As the Hunger Games fan account Panem Propaganda said, “If I had a nickel for every time a Sunrise on the Reaping cast member played the son of their older Hunger Games counterpart in a completely unrelated movie, I’d have two nickels. Which isn’t a lot, but it’s weird that it happened twice,” referring to the castings of Beetee and Plutarch.

Beetee was the winner of the 34th Hunger Games from District 3 who was reaped for the 75th Hunger Games in Catching Fire. The new film delves into his heartbreaking backstory, involving the fate of his son, Ampert, as well as his secret plan with Haymitch to disrupt the games.

Iris Apatow is a 24-year-old actress who previously appeared in This Is 40, The Bubble, and Knocked Up. She is the daughter of actress Leslie Mann and producer Judd Apatow, the sister of Euphoria star Maude Apatow, and the girlfriend of White Lotus star Sam Nivola. She plays Proserpina Trinket, Effie’s younger sister and member of the District 12 prep team.

Edvin Ryding is a 23-year-old Swedish actor who previously appeared in 28 Years Later and Young Royals. He plays Vitus, Proserpina’s friend and sidekick on the District 12 prep team.

Whitney Peak is a 23-year-old Ugandan-Canadian actress best known for her role as Zoya in the 2021 Gossip Girl reboot. She plays Lenore Dove, the Covey singer and love of Haymitch's life.

Though her character is unique to Sunrise on the Reaping, Lenore Dove is rumored to be the daughter of either Maude Ivory or Lucy Gray Baird. Fans can’t help but notice the resemblance between Peak and Rachel Zegler.

McKenna Grace is a 20-year-old actress best known for her roles in The Handmaid's Tale, Ghostbusters: Afterlife, and Young Sheldon. While young, she has been acting since she was five, and she has had at least 34 film and television roles.

She plays Maysilee Donner, the spoiled mean girl who turns into a star when she is reaped for the 50th Hunger Games. She has a twin sister, Merrilee (whose daughter later gifts Katniss the Mockingjay pin), and is friends with Asterid March, Katniss's mother. She allies herself with Haymitch during the game, and the two share a sibling-like bond.

Ben Wang is a 29-year-old actor best known for his roles in Karate Kid: Legends and American Born Chinese.

He plays Wyatt Callow, Haymitch's fellow District 12 boy tribute. The Callow family runs the gambling scene in District 12, placing bets on the Hunger Games tributes each year.

Wang’s Instagram is @definitelynotbenwang.

Glenn Close is a 79-year-old Tony, Emmy, and Golden Globe winner best known for her roles in Fatal Attraction, 101 Dalmatians, and The Deliverance.

She plays Drusilla Sickle, the District 12 tributes’ escort. Drusilla is known for her many cosmetic surgeries that poorly attempt to disguise her age. She and Maysilee are the biggest divas in Panem, and their personalities clash disastrously.

Billy Porter is a 57-year-old Emmy, Tony, and Grammy Award–winning actor, singer, composer, director, and playwright.

He plays Magno Stift, the incompetent stylist for the District 12 tributes. His personal style involves wearing a plethora of live reptiles, and his hobbies include licking frogs for the psychedelic effects.

Molly McCann is a young Irish actress who previously appeared in Bad Sisters, Herself, and Rosie. She plays Louella McCoy, a young girl from District 12 who Haymitch views as a little sister and nicknamed “Sweetheart.” Fans were quick to notice McCann's resemblance to Katniss (Jennifer Laurence), the only other character who Haymitch refers to as Sweetheart.

McCann’s Instagram is @mollymccannactress.

Iona Bell is a young English actress who plays Louella’s uncanny lookalike, Lou Lou.

Her Instagram is @iona_bell_official_actor.

Kara Tointon is a 43-year-old English actress who previously appeared in EastEnders, The Teacher, and Too Good to Be True. She plays Willamae Abernathy, Haymitch's mother.

#6841. The Best Gingerbread Perfumes to Shop for a Cozy, Elevated Winter Scent

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

These are the festive, spicy perfumes worth wearing now.

’Tis the season to embrace all things cozy, and that includes gingerbread. The scent isn’t just wafting from our ovens and favorite coffee shops anymore. It’s showing up in our everyday lives, too, as gingerbread fragrances grow more popular.

In perfume, gingerbread is a gourmand accord: warm ginger, molasses, and cinnamon, usually rounded out with vanilla or woods. Ginger is a clever ingredient. Perfumers often use it as a top note, where it adds sparkle and energy, and it pairs beautifully with pepper, citrus, rich woods, and amber. That’s why a good gingerbread perfume can feel cozy without turning into a candle.

Leave it to Squishmallows to create the cutest gingerbread fragrance, complete with a vintage-inspired Cam the Cat bottle. This cozy scent blends ginger, coffee biscuit, and caramel for a sweet, snuggly treat that smells like a warm bakery on a chilly morning.

Jo Malone’s Ginger Biscuit is back for the holiday season. This warm, spicy scent blends ginger, cinnamon, and roasted hazelnut, giving your fragrance wardrobe a cozy, bakery-fresh addition that’s perfect for chilly days.

For on-the-go delight, this travel-size spray delivers warm sweetness with a hint of citrus zest. Slip it into your bag for a quick spritz of holiday cheer whenever you need it.

A scent that’s sure to put you in a festive mood, Baked Gingerbread & Brown Sugar makes holiday spritzing feel extra sweet, cozy, and celebratory.

Although it’s not entirely inspired by gingerbread, this Dior Spice Blend fragrance feels luxuriously rich, with notes of fresh rum, black pepper, and a ginger accord.

Freshly made gingerbread dough comes to mind with every whiff of this unisex fragrance. Described as a golden embrace, the scent feels familiar yet unexpected. It’s heartwarming yet sensual, and it lingers on the skin long after the holidays are over.

“I saw you on 8th Avenue drinking a smoothie, and you paid me no mind.” Jennifer Lawrence typed these words on her keyboard, and they have lived rent-free in Connor Wood’s mind ever since. “I don’t know how to feel about it. I did not expect that to go viral. I don’t...I don’t really get it,” she admitted, before Josh jokingly chimed in, “I don’t know what any of those words mean.”

#6830. These Evergreen Perfumes Are Perfect for the Holidays (And Won’t Make You Smell Like a Candle)

Turns out there are a million ways to wear pine, balsam, and juniper notes.

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

As much as I can appreciate a good gourmand or fruity floral, I am a spicy, woody scent girl through and through. And when the wintertime hits, there’s nothing better than the fresh fragrance of coniferous evergreens: pine, spruce, juniper, cypress, cedar, hemlock... if a plant is spiky and verdant, I want to smell it. Until recently, these notes were something I associated with candles—lovely to burn while I was watching a movie by the fire, not for spritzing onto myself during the day.

But, in the past few years, a veritable sleigh-load of evergreen-forward perfumes have launched, and I’ve deeply enjoyed almost every one I’ve sniffed. So, in the spirit of the holiday season, I’ve chatted with some experts about why this particular subcategory of woody fragrance seems to be so popular now and what notes pair best with evergreen for a wearable scent, plus rounded up a few of our perfumes with notes like pine, fir, cypress, spruce, and juniper that are perfect for this most festive time of year.

This rise in evergreen notes aligns quite well with what’s going on in the rest of the perfume world. “When it comes to perfume, this year has been all about touching grass, getting back to nature and living IRL,” says Steven Gontarski, senior manager of brand and public relations at retailer Luckyscent and host of the It’s Just Perfume podcast. “These scents remind us that we're actually living beings with a relationship to the natural world and not just disembodied fingers scrolling on endless screens.”

While we saw nature-inspired perfumes that emulate the sun, juicy berries, even the stinkiest flower in the world launch this fall, so it make sense that the plants most associated with winter are getting their time in the scent spotlight right now. There’s also something inherently cozy about pine.

“Evergreen is what comfort smells like when it isn’t dessert,” says Jeniece Trizzino, vice president of innovation and physical product at fragrance companies Scentbird and Drift. Sweet gourmands have held on to the “most comforting scent category” for a while, but Trizzino agrees that people are seeking a connection to nature now.

“Old trees, forest floor, damp earth: These scents give people a way to reset,” she says.

Evergreen notes are fairly versatile, though they tend to find their way into scents that lean, for lack of a better word, unisex rather than what we think of as traditionally feminine fragrances.

Gontarski loves notes of evergreen paired with incense, which adds a smokiness to the scent. He’s a fan of bright citrus notes too, saying they create “a perfect counterpoint” to resinous notes like pine.

“The best pairings put evergreen somewhere real,” Trizzino says—in other words, these woodsy notes pair best with other scents you can find in nature. She loves them with herbs like mint, basil, or eucalyptus (a different type of evergreen, BTW) and damp notes like moss, wet stone, or ocean air. If you’re still seeking the warmth that’s so common with winter gourmands, Trizzino says evergreen also pairs well with amber, musk, tobacco, and whiskey notes.

Kicking off this list with what I feel comfortable calling the most-likely-to-please-everyone evergreen scent. Yes, the juniper and cedar make for an undoubtedly pine-y fragrance, but it’s paired with warm, creamy notes of cardamom, suede, bourbon, and vanilla that almost bring it into gourmand territory.

This year, Nest turned its best-selling Holiday candle into a perfume you can wear. I’ll admit, I was skeptical that it would turn out well, but the brand really nailed it: The scent is much juicier than the candle, thanks to grapefruit and cranberry, but still has that familiar balsam note.

If you like a juicier scent story, surprise! There’s even an evergreen fragrance for you. This one goes dual meaning on the name “pine,” with notes of pineapple and pine tree, plus some florals and citrus. The result is something that truly works year-round.

This scent smells way more expensive than its under-$50 price tag. Evergreen really leads the charge too: There’s both fir balsam and torched cedar, paired with patchouli and vanilla for a smoky, warm fragrance.

Come for the gorgeous bottle, stay for the invigorating blend of pine, bergamot, eucalyptus, and cypress. It’s not quite as good as a Mediterranean vacation, but it’ll do until I save up enough miles for another trip.

Both my partner and I love this savory gourmand scent. Coriander mixes with juniper for a bright, peppery opening, but it dries down to a musky, spicy, floral thanks to clove, clary sage, magnolia, and mace.

“My all-time favorite is Santal 33,” Trizzino says. “I like woods that are warm and a little creamy.” You’ve almost definitely smelled Santal 33 before, but you might not have realized it has an evergreen (cedar) at its heart.

This aquatic evergreen scent from trendy fragrance brand Noyz is similar in vibes to Santal 33, but a bit more subtle on the skin. It really does smell like the inside of a spa you’d be thrilled to visit on your shittiest of days.

This is a perfume that made me totally forget I’m not typically a fan of rose scents. D’Orsay pairs the flower with a cypress, vetiver, and patchouli base that all adds up to a fragrance that feels both familiar and brand new—like an acoustic cover of your favorite song from high school.

Another cult classic that’s packed with evergreen notes, Gypsy Water opens with juniper berries and bergamot, has pine needles and incense at its heart, then finishes off with a cozy amber-vanilla-sandalwood base.

Fresh, laundry day-inspired scents are another big trend right now. Indie brand Cayla Gray’s take is (as the name implies) quite floral, but there’s white cedar at the base that makes this fragrance smell like walking in the woods on a crisp winter morning.

Nightchild is one of Gontarski’s favorite examples of an evergreen scent. In it, “incense pairs beautifully with evergreen notes,” he says. “Perfumer Michael Nordstrand tells a story of moonlight penetrating a forest canopy with frankincense mimicking its silver light.”

Orphéon is a classic spicy evergreen scent that’s beloved by many already. Named for a Parisian jazz bar that was popular in the 1960s, the vibe is sexy, smoky, and a little bit mysterious. The note of ginger gives it a sweet-spicy touch that you won’t find in any of the other fragrances on this list.

This is a (literally) fresh take on evergreen, perfect for someone who’s seeking a scent that’s light and airy rather than dark and spicy. It’s packed full of familiar herbs; fir, balsam and cypress; plus fig and olive for a touch of savory gourmand goodness.

If you’re looking for a scent that combines both sea and forest, this unisex scent from Giardini di Toscana will suit you just fine. It’s a marine fragrance that blends a little bit of citrus, a little bit of cyprus, and some salty notes for a fragrance that smells like the color turquoise.

Another of Gontarski’s favorites, G.494 has lots of citrus. “Notes of bitter orange and mandarin create a perfect counterpoint to the balsamic resins and evergreen needles,” he says. “It’s clean and invigorating in an understated way.”

After most recently serving as content director at Allure (2019-2025) and executive editor at Bustle (2014-2019), she is now fully freelance. In addition to Cosmo, her writing has appeared in Allure, The New York Times, Marie Claire, Business of Fashion, Playboy, NH Magazine, and more. Kara lives in New Hampshire with her dog, four cats, and a rotating cast of foster animals. #6827. How The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping Showcases Effie Trinket’s Fashion Evolution

Costume designer Bina Daigeler shares the inspiration behind the fashion in the new film.

If you follow red carpet fashion, these past few weeks, it’s been impossible to ignore Elle Fanning’s method dressing madness as Effie Trinket. From toy ballerina music box headpieces by Robert Wun fit for a fairy tale to makeup compact dresses (and matching headwear) from Givenchy, the fashion story is strong. All roads lead back to her character as young Effie Trinket from The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping, which is making its theater debut on November 20. Based on Suzanne Collins’s 2025 novel of the same name, it is set 24 years before The Hunger Games and 40 years after The Ballad of Songbirds & Snakes. It begins the morning of the 50th Hunger Games, with a 16-year-old Haymitch Abernathy (Joseph Zada) and Effie Trinket as a young stylist in the Capitol.

Bina Daigeler is the costume designer behind the new film, and with her team, she oversaw the creation of every single outfit from scratch, with a few helping accessories pulled from vintage specialists. She began with a mood board that focused on the aesthetic differences between the 13 districts while keeping in mind the personalities of the actors. Drusilla Sickle (Glenn Close) had a devoted board full of Alexander McQueen, John Galliano, and Vivienne Westwood, for instance. “It was really like old couture fashion,” Daigeler tells ELLE. “But pieces that, for me, would age very well, you know, that would keep up in time, because I think that is very important when you work on such a famous franchise; that you design for the future. That it’s still interesting in 10 years.”

“Working with Glenn Close, she really came to me and said, ‘I need to find a thread through all the costumes that are made for me, so that I can understand it,’” says Daigeler. “That was super important for her.” Daigeler wove everything together with “cool accessories to give her the strength,” and one very special finale look. “She wanted to have a big finale, you know, like a finale where she really feels like ‘I’m the winner. it’s nothing about Haymitch, it's all about me.’” Think: lots of red.

Daigeler also referenced Woodstock, Studio 54, the late ’60s and early ’70s. “I tried really to have a strong concept that has to do with the personality of each character,” she says. Unsurprisingly, given Effie Trinket’s predilection for fashion as a stylist, she was a big focus for Daigeler. “For example, young Effie Trinket, how did she become the Effie Trinket that Elizabeth Banks was? Here she is younger, she is fresh, she doesn’t have a lot of money or a fashion background. So it must be something that looked a little bit vintage that could be done at home, a mixture of fabrics, of textures, of color, something very young and fresh and fun.”

Early on in the film, we see Effie Trinket’s fashion as improvisational. But Daigeler intentionally showcases her ascension from fashion fan to fashion icon throughout the film. Daigeler says that Effie’s outfits all had to have fun elements. “The black kimono with the flowers and patchwork little waistcoat with a flowery blouse and all the color combinations were very strange, but it worked together,” she says. She also mentions her blue denim, Vivienne Westwood-inspired piece with huge sleeves as being the epitome of young Effie Trinket style.

“The most important thing was, for Effie, that she looks fun, but also free, because she is in the Capitol, but is still the freest and the most positive and optimistic character,” says Daigeler. “She doesn’t look like damaged and depressed and she had to come in and cheer them up and she definitely came with a solution. Our heroes somehow could trust her and they were so happy to see her. All the other characters from the Capitol, you really don’t know who you can trust.” Therefore, their aesthetic is darker while Effie is lighter and brighter.

Look out for the fashion Easter eggs throughout the film, like Effie Trinket’s butterfly crystal rings, which were just some of the pieces that Daigeler sourced vintage from one of her favorite specialists from around the world. Elsewhere, Haymitch has his own fashion revolution in a quieter way. “The way also how Haymitch wears the clothes and how they were aged and the colors, everything just comes together,” says Daigeler. “I think the audience will find, or I hope, that they find that there are things that resemble and they recognize it.”

Likewise for Caesar Flickerman (Kieran Culkin), the longtime Capitol television host, there’s an emphasis on fashionable suits. “It was very, very important that he can move because he is a very physical actor whom moves a lot, so he needed space in his suits,” says Daigeler. “Suits also had to give him power.”

And for Snow (Ralph Fiennes), too, suiting is used to convey power. “He’s very particular about fabrics, about the cut. So I went to a tailor in London, who I have known for many, many years,” she says. “Snow’s suits we developed really like a little bit, World War II military cuts, with a very strong shoulder and a very strong silhouette and these heavy fabrics, and the right colors. But then we gave him the fantasy through hand-embroidered lapels. It had also the hint to his rose that he always wears, and he has somehow always loved flowers.”

Fashion is both spectacle and a survival tool in The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping. The odds are forever in fashion’s favor.

A box of Jones Road will instantly make you the favorite.

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

I am the ultimate beauty Santa amongst my friends and family. Beauty gifts can be notoriously difficult to give—I mean, there are always questions of shade, hair type, skin type, and scent preferences. And for some, it might feel awkward to give someone, say, a wrinkle cream or foundation, no matter how fancy and expensive it is. But as a beauty editor, it’s basically my duty to give away the best beauty gifts every year. I’ve gifted LED masks, hair tools, high-end skincare, and even the odd designer nail polish or bodycare. Among it all, though, the most excited I’ve ever seen my family get is opening a box filled with Jones Road Beauty.

Created by makeup industry legend Bobbi Brown, Jones Road is makeup, but better. All of the brand’s products perform easily, with minimal fuss, making it a go-to for anyone who isn’t a whiz with a brush but wants to look like one. And it’s particularly fantastic for mature skin, thanks to creamy, moisturizing formulas that don’t cling to texture or settle into fine lines.

While the brand runs its annual Black Friday deal, I’m sharing a few of my favorite products below, with quick reviews. Keep scrolling for my favorite lipsticks, lightweight foundation, cream blush, mascara, and more.

This is my favorite tinted moisturizer of all time, and it performs just as well in the winter when my skin is dry as it does when I’m oilier in the summer. It feels like I’m just wearing a lightweight moisturizer, but it adds just enough coverage to conceal dark spots and blemishes.

There’s no flaking, ever, with this mascara. But it still adds a good amount of volume and length without any clumps.

This went viral when it first launched, prompting many of my family members to ask me if it was worth the hype. #6818. Taylor Swift’s Burgundy Wine Manicure Is the Winter Nail Color to Know

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

This week at the CMA Awards, Taylor Swift’s nails were so scarlet, they were practically “maroon.” The singer sported short nails painted a shade of deep red that evoked a tall, thin wine glass, and the manicure went with all three of her outfits of the night. It’s not the first time she’s given us nail inspiration—she recently had “gold fairy dust nails” that stole the show at the 2026 Academy Museum Gala. But this new color is the perfect shade for cooler weather.

“I would describe this as a classic burgundy manicure,” says Vikki Swan, award-winning nail educator and founder of Vikki Swan Nail Academy in Los Angeles. “The deep wine-red shade creates a timeless, elegant look. It’s a great example of how a simple, solid color can make a statement without any additional nail art.”

The color also isn’t completely opaque—but it also isn’t translucent. Jin Soon Choi, manicurist and founder of Jinsoon, describes the polish as a “crelly.” “The crelly finish combines the richness of a cream [nail polish] with the softness of a jelly,” she says. “It gives this classic color a youthful, modern twist while keeping it soft and effortless.”

Swift’s manicure fits right into winter nail trends. “We’re seeing richer, darker colors come forward: black cherry, garnet, burgundy, and deep wine,” says Max Estrada, founder of eNailCouture. “They’re luxurious without relying on glitter or extra embellishment, and they work beautifully with winter fashion. It is the sort of color that gives a simple manicure presence.” Swift isn’t the only one—Jennifer Lopez and Anne Hathaway have both stepped out with similar manicures recently.

If you want to try it yourself, here’s how to re-create it. “Start with a clean manicure, shaping the nails short with softly rounded or soft-square edges and gently pushing back the cuticles,” Choi says. “Apply a base coat, followed by two thin layers of spiced red ‘crelly’ polish to build color without looking heavy. End with a top coat for an ultra-shiny finish that makes the color look lively and fresh.” And if you really want to nod to Swift, you can do it all while listening to “Red.”

#6817. Which Christmas Tree Is Right for Your Home? Experts Break It Down

Consider this your shortcut to finding the perfect holiday centerpiece.

Holiday decorating is supposed to be a joyous event, but picking the right tree can be surprisingly complicated. There’s a lot of height, circumference, and branch density variation—some people like a full, fluffy look, while others opt for a more minimal tree—and don’t even entice us with the real versus faux debate.

The truth is, any tree can be beautiful in the right setting, so if you’re feeling unsure about which one fits your home, we’ve created the ultimate guide on which tree is right for your holiday centerpiece. Ahead, we're sharing the most popular types of Christmas trees for every household.

The Fraser fir is one of the most popular types of Christmas tree, especially for households east of the Rocky Mountains. Firs, in general, are some of the most common trees grown for the holiday season, says Ward Dilmore, founder of Petrus Landscaping in California. There's a good reason for this: They have a perfect balance of deep green color and even, stiff branching, he says, which makes them a breeze to decorate.

Canaan fir trees often get compared to Fraser and balsam firs, thanks to their similar growing patterns and branch density, but this variety has a major benefit above the rest: needle retention. It sheds less than other firs, which means less cleanup for you.

Often a home for many birds, including woodpeckers, the eastern white pine is fluffy and fast-growing. With foliage that's thin, delicate, and silvery-green, this tree is best paired with lightweight ornaments and minimal decorations.

A classic type of Christmas tree, the noble fir is native to the Cascade and Pacific Coast mountain ranges of the Northwestern United States. With strong branches, this tree ensures your precious family heirlooms won't be at risk of falling, and the long peak is ideal for an oversized tree topper.

The Arizona cypress is the most drought-tolerant evergreen out there—with the right care, this type of Christmas tree can last longer than other evergreens. With its thick, layered branches and lacy silver-blue foliage, this tree is a beautiful alternative to the silver fir.

You’ll find this bushy type of Christmas tree primarily in Southern states, according to the Real Christmas Tree Board. Thanks to its stout branches, it should have no problem holding up your heaviest ceramic ornaments. It also has a piney fragrance and keeps its needles well throughout the season, as long as you give it plenty of water to drink.

Also known as a red fir, these striking trees are one of the rarest types of Christmas trees, says horticulturist and botanical designer Nathan Heinrich. They're slow-growing, and only a few farmers and harvesters have permits in California and Oregon to harvest them in the wild each year, he explains. "The reason these trees make such perfect Christmas trees is because they have such perfectly spaced, open, elegant branches that make hanging ornaments and lights easy," Heinrich says.

Like Miracle on 34th Street and peppermint hot chocolate, the balsam fir is a holiday classic. It's also the most fragrant of all types of Christmas trees, according to the Real Christmas Tree Board. You can find these at Christmas tree farms in Canada, New England, the upper Great Lakes, and Pennsylvania, along with lots of retail locations east of the Rocky Mountains.

The Scotch pine often flies under the radar, but it's a designer-favorite Christmas tree choice, and it has great needle retention. "With vibrant green and durable needles that resist shedding, it's low maintenance," says New York designer Artem Kropovinsky of interior design studio Arsight. "Its robust branches accommodate even the heaviest ornaments, and its dense appearance gives rooms a fuller, festive look."

The trees you see in lots often have been pruned and sheared into the quintessential Christmas tree shape, explains Lisa Tadewaldt, an ISA-certified arborist and owner of Portland, Oregon–based Urban Forest Professionals. She recommends getting a permit and cutting a tree that's native to your area down in the forest. In the Pacific Northwest, her favorite tree in its natural form is a grand fir. "It is going to give more Charlie Brown vibes than Hallmark Christmas vibes, but it has a flat needle pattern that gives it an air of elegance that balances out the thinner, more homespun branching pattern," she says.

With its signature bluish needles, the robust blue spruce has a full, triangular shape, says Kropovinsky. "Apart from its signature color, this tree releases a fresh scent similar to frosty woodlands," he says. Bonus for pet owners: "Given the tree's pointed foliage, pets often steer clear of it."

With its flexible needles and twigs, the Eastern red cedar doesn't hold ornaments all that well. But don’t let that deter you! If your heart is set on this hardy juniper, it looks great draped with lights, says ISA-certified master arborist Blake Watkins, operations partner at Monster Tree Service.

The Douglas fir is a type of Christmas tree that is synonymous with the Pacific Northwest, but is available at holiday tree markets nationwide. It's also one of the more affordable types of Christmas trees you can find. "Dark green foliage and hanging needles make this tree gorgeous even without ornaments," Watkins says.

The concolor fir (also called the white fir) is the type of tree you'd like to sidle up next to with a mug of mulled wine. While many trees have pine scents, this one actually gives off a citrus aroma that has garnered it some devoted fans, according to the Michigan State University Extension. Looks-wise, its branches have long, silvery, bluish-green needles.

Follow House Beautiful on Instagram and TikTok.

Meghan Shouse is House Beautiful's Digital Editor, and she's been with the team since September 2023. On top of being the magazine's go-to Waco expert, Meghan also touches on quite a few other verticals in the digital space, like hot news content featuring your favorite HGTV stars, home design inspiration, and the latest interior TikTok trends. However, her favorite pieces are the ones that start conversations, such as stories about resale value and whether or not the Solo cup is chic. Before becoming a home writer, Meghan worked in the fashion industry for two years, writing for Harper's Bazaar after graduating from Iowa State University with a bachelor's degree in apparel merchandising. When she's not interviewing interior designers about home trends, you can find Meghan reading a fictional thriller with her cat in her lap, planning themed parties, or strolling through flea markets searching for chunky candle holders and vintage bookends.

Blake Watkins is an ISA board-certified Master Arborist and an ASCA-registered Consulting Arborist located in Sacramento, CA. He is the National Director of Plant Health Care for Monster Tree Service and is the owner of Arborist Blake Watkins LLC.

#6814. Pink Hair Is Back and Everyone From SZA to Adéla Are Making a Case for It

Remember when Kylie Jenner was changing her hair color about every other week, Tumblr was overflowing with pastel-haired girlies, and we were all contemplating whether we could pull off pink hair? Well, dust off your 2016 Pinterest boards, bb, because pink hair is officially making a comeback. But this time, it’s not just King Kylie leading the charge. SZA, Adéla, and even Martha Stewart have been putting their own spins on the color, proving that there’s no one way (or age!) to enter your pink hair era. “Since pink is not a natural hair color, you don’t really have to abide by any typical color analysis rules,” says colorist Emily Claire, which means you have free range to do whatever the heck you’d like.

And the range is range-ing. From electric pink to soft rose gold, I promise there’s a rosy hue for you. Personally, I’m loving this return to hair color that’s just fun—and I’m not the only one. “Recently my clients with pink hair have been requesting baby pink and deep berry pink,” says colorist Cara Craig. Plus, Cosmo called out pink as a top fall hair color trend this year. So, whether you’re ready to go full-on Barbie or want to experiment with a subtle rosy tint, there’s a pink hair idea with your name on it. Ahead, we’re breaking it all down, including how to choose your shade, what to ask for at the salon, and how to keep your pink hair looking fresh.

Short answer: Yes. There’s a truly pink hair color for everyone. “I have clients asking for all shades of pink, from blush and rose gold to hot pink to magenta,” says hairstylist Alyssa Chapa.

Pink is surprisingly so versatile, so there are plenty of ways to make it work for you. “In my opinion, pink is a neutral. I think of fashion colors as cousins to classic shades,” says Craig. “So if you are a blonde, consider baby pink. If you are a brunette you’ll want to move to darker pinks and fuchsias. If you want to feel like a redhead then wear rose golds and warm pinks.” She also recommends a violet pink if you prefer keeping things cool-toned.

Wondering about how your skin tone will look with pink hair? “I’m personally of the opinion that there is no right or wrong shade for your skin tone,” says Chapa, but if it’s really important to you, here are her tips for finding the best hair color for your skin tone. “Start by figuring out your undertones,” she says. “I like using the veins on the inside of the wrist; if you have blue or purple veins then you usually have cool undertones, green veins mean warm undertones, and a mixture of both is a neutral undertone.” Then, it’s pretty easy from there: Match your undertones to the shade of pink, which your colorist will be able to help out with (think baby or icy pink for cool tones, peach or salmon pink for warm tones, that kind of thing).

First things first: Bring reference photos to your colorist—we’ve handily included some pics throughout this story for ya. After all, Adéla’s vivid pink and Stewart’s subtle rose gold hair require very different approaches, so you’ll want to make sure you’re specific about what shade you’re after.

“For most people, unless you happen to be naturally blonde, the process would involve lightening your hair to the desired level with bleach, then toning it to the desired pink with a direct dye,” says Claire.

Need some specific pink hair ideas? These are colorist-approved: “One of my favorite ways to wear pink lately is over classic blonde highlights,” says Craig. “I also love a pink double process (e.g. Adéla).” Double process just means a two-step coloring method—your colorist will first bleach/lighten your hair, then apply the pink color. You can also go with chunky highlights, “especially a halo hairline where the pink frames the face,” adds Chapa. “Mixing chunky pink highlights in with shades of red or brown to create a kind of calico effect is also popular online.”

Now for the not-so-fun part: Pink hair doesn’t necessarily equal low-maintenance hair. “No matter the tone, you’re going to have to refresh it in four to six weeks if you want it to stay pink,” says Chapa. If you want to go as low-maintenance as possible, a softer pink is the way to go, notes Craig.

For a more natural-looking grow-out or to keep your hair color easy, “your alternatives are to choose an already muted color like a dusty rose or mauve and let it fade to blonde or brunette, or to invest in an at-home color depositing treatment to maintain your pink until your next salon visit,” Chapa says.

You can help extend your color by washing less frequently, using color-safe shampoo and conditioner, limiting heat styling, and incorporating a color-depositing mask or at-home hair gloss. “I always recommend my clients use a pink-tinted shampoo or conditioner to keep up with the color between appointments at home,” says Claire.

Catharine Malzahn is a contributing beauty writer at Cosmo. She’s covered all things skincare, makeup, hair, and more, including trending haircuts and hair colors you have to try.

Catharine Malzahn is a contributing beauty writer at Cosmopolitan and was previously the beauty assistant at Good Housekeeping, Woman's Day, and Prevention.

#6813. The Tutu Is the Tactile Miracle Fashion Needs Right Now

The tutu’s fashion legacy just keeps going.

Distinctly fluffy, feminine, and over-the-top, the tutu has an unrivaled fashion legacy. Is it any wonder that in the past few weeks, celebrities have been joyfully committed to wearing the frilly staple? It may be peak Nutcracker season, but even so, Balletcore will never die. The aesthetic has experienced its original iteration (dating back to the 1800s), its first fashion wave in the ’50s and again in the ’80s, followed by a darker take, a more grounded look, and yet it still persists. At the center of it is the tutu: Nicole Kidman recently wore a Matthieu Blazy Chanel dress with a perfectly layered tutu tulle confection of a skirt that would give Carrie Bradshaw a run for her money. Elsewhere, Rosalía, Tinashe, and Olivia Rodrigo have all recently donned the tutu. The look right now reads as more punky Y2K or ’80s couture.

The spring/summer 2027 runways were littered with echoes of the tutu, too. Both Chanel and Dior showcased voluminous skirts with tutu-like shapes; Dior’s were made of cut fabric petals. Rising label Agro Studio integrated ethereal watercolor tutus into its recent collection. And it’s impossible to forget Cecilie Bahnsen’s undying dedication to the garment. Her fall/winter 2026 collection was a tribute to movement and dance and repositioned the tutu as an everyday item versus occasionwear, pairing it with fleeces and down jackets as live dancers wove throughout the venue wearing tutus. “They brought a beautiful femininity and softness to the more utilitarian pieces, creating an interesting contrast,” Bahnsen tells ELLE. “And as the dancers moved through the show, the tutus came to life, adding a wonderful sense of volume, lightness, and movement to the silhouettes.”

In 2026, wearing a tutu is an undeniable endorphin booster. Perhaps it’s deeply and satisfyingly impractical, the exact opposite of wearing a pair of denim jeans and a T-shirt or button-down, the kinds of items that typically dominate capsule wardrobes. “I love the femininity, the sculptural quality, and the beautiful bounce and movement of a tutu,” adds Bahnsen. “There is something so joyful, light, and full of positive energy about it.”

Given the tutu’s broad history, it’s no wonder vintage tutus are experiencing a bit of a revival too. According to Haile Lidow, founder of Lidow Archive, there’s been a surge in demand. “The tutus I can’t keep in stock right now are lighter and more ballerina-focused,” she tells ELLE. “I have this David Dalrymple for Patricia Field white tutu skirt that was made to be like the famous Carrie Bradshaw title sequence look, and that piece has been renting out constantly. I’ve also been obsessed with tattered tulle lately and the line it tows between innocence and darkness.”

Like Bahnsen, she sees an inherent joy in the silhouette and also links the current wide appeal to the extra textural feeling. “There’s something very tactile about tutus, and in an age of digital-everything, I think we’re craving pieces that seem to reach out and ask us to interact with them IRL,” she adds. In a season of pared-back dressing, the tutu’s frothy excess feels like a joyful rebellion.

#6811. 27 Charming Christmas Towns That Look Like a Real-Life Holiday Movie

Think twinkling lights, towering trees, and Santa-themed bike rides.

The holiday season is in full swing, with many halls and houses already decked to impress in garland and fairy lights. But as you wrap gifts, prepare to host, and begin marathoning holiday movies, it can feel like the joyous season is coming to an end much too soon. We can't blame you if you're still wanting more from this merry time of year, and we've come up with a solution. There are plenty of beautiful Christmas towns across the country to fill your heart with peace, joy, and nostalgia—you just have to know where to look.

These towns are more than just beautifully decorated—they’re truly immersive, offering experiences from Santa-themed bike rides to orchestrated light shows and winter bar crawls. Below, we've rounded up the 27 most charming Christmas towns from all over the country, so no matter where you live, you'll be close enough to find a bit of extra cheer this year.

This picturesque mountain town serves as the perfect holiday backdrop for more reasons than just the ever-present blanket of fluffy white snow. Each year, the valley is transformed into a Christmas town with seasonal theatrical performances, Gunnison’s Night of Lights, a tree lighting ceremony, and even an ice sculpting event.

Ever since the formation of this city on Christmas Eve in 1741, Bethlehem, Pennsylvania, has been rooted in the holiday spirit. From Christmas markets to historical tours to horse-drawn carriage rides, there's no doubt this town won't feel magical. And make sure to stop by the historic Sayre Mansion, pictured here, to check out their elegant holiday decor.

Annapolis is known as one of the best cities during the holiday season thanks to its winter weather and historic downtown. If you're lucky enough to visit this Christmas town, make sure you spend time bargain shopping until midnight, checking out the spectacular light display in Sandy Point State Park, and viewing the Yacht Club Light Parade.

Chicago is exceptionally beautiful during the Christmas time. From the seemingly endless strings of lights to the Christkindlmarket, which is free to enter and so charming, the Windy City has many offerings during the holiday season, making it one of the best Christmas towns to visit. Plus, you don't want to miss ice skating or any Christmas-themed museum exhibits.

If you want to do something Christmas-y almost every single day of December, then Newport, Rhode Island, might just be the place to go. To create the perfect coastal Christmas, this town offers a month-long celebration that's been going strong for 54 years. From holiday bike rides to Medieval festivals to winter afternoon teas, there's so much to do in this Christmas town.

You'll feel as if you've traveled back to Charles Dickens's time when you walk through Franklin's historic Christmas festival. Characters from A Christmas Carol and Oliver Twist come to life throughout the weekend-long festivities. Roam the Victorian Village and try Victorian-era Christmas treats like sugar plums while listening to carolers.

A classic ski town, Park City offers Christmas markets, festive decorations, and a Christmas Eve parade lit by torches—plus spectacular views of the mountains are around every corner.

In the heart of Virginia horse country, you'll find one of the most festive Christmas towns. Cheer on the town's hunting hounds while sipping hard cider and wander the streets in search of vintage Christmas ornaments.

During Kennebunkport's annual Christmas Prelude, the town puts on a very New England celebration: You can watch Santa Claus arrive by lobster boat, go on seashore holiday trolley rides, and enjoy wine tastings and lobster bakes. Not to mention, the town is covered in Christmas lights and garlands that make it the perfect festive photo backdrop.

For a dreamy and unique experience, head to Santa Fe. One of the most anticipated celebrations is the Canyon Road Farolito Walk, which is when thousands of farolitos—luminaries made of paper bags, sand, and tea candles—cast a glow on the gardens and old buildings that line the road. You also can get your white Christmas fix by visiting Ski Santa Fe.

As one of the oldest towns in the country, Portsmouth is a charming New England destination that celebrates the holidays with a Vintage Christmas Celebration. Visit the historic village of Strawberry Banke or wander around the charming shopping districts in this historic Christmas town.

Get a Hallmark movie feel while experiencing a taste of Denmark in California's Solvang village, located in Santa Ynez Valley wine country. The destination hosts Julefest, a month-long holiday celebration with live entertainment, a parade, and a night market.

Koziar's Christmas Village in Bernville is one of the top attractions in Pennsylvania. With more than a million lights adorning buildings, barns, and the landscape—and a reflective lake that doubles the beauty—it's hard to beat the town's display.

This Bavarian-style village is charming any time of year! December festivities include a display of Santa costumes from all around the world, a gingerbread house competition, carolers, and plenty of photo ops.

Of course, New York City isn't a small Christmas town, but it is extra magical during the holidays. From the massive tree at Rockefeller Center to the various Christmas markets in the city parks with ice skating, shops, and more, you can't beat the Big Apple's holiday spirit.

Nicknamed the Lighted Poinsettia Capital of Texas, this town brings major holiday spirit every year. Visit during December when the lighted trail in Comanche Trail Park is aglow all month long. Check out Hotel Settles for its elegant Art Deco lodging.

During the holiday season, each home in Historic Williamsburg lights one candle, a tradition that dates back to the Revolutionary War era. The cobblestone streets and colonial holiday traditions such as caroling by torchlight make this destination a must, especially for history buffs.

This Christmas town stays decked out year-round, so if you're craving holiday spirit in July, you'll still find candy-cane posts, boughs of holly, and a Santa Claus house with live reindeer!

Christmas without the cold? We've got you covered! In addition to abundant sunshine and festive holiday decor, you'll also have plenty of history to explore in St. Augustine, America's oldest city.

Want to take a ride on the Polar Express? Visit Durango during the holidays. The historic mining town offers a Polar Express–inspired trip that your kiddos will flip over.

#6804. Yes, Katie and Victor Were Flirting on ‘The Traitors: New Blood’—and They Dished All About Their First Date

Nothing says romance like trying not to get murdered on reality TV.

The Traitors may be a game of deceit, but Victor Vollbrechthausen and Katie Fites’ flirtation? That was apparently very real.

As the first civilian-only season of The Traitors: New Blood came to a close, host Alan Cumming asked Katie, “Is there a possible date on the cards for you and Victor?”

She seemed tainted by the fact that Victor had banished her, jokingly saying, “No date for you,” until he kept the banter going and claimed that he “earned it” after finding out she was a Traitor. Now, the star-crossed Faithful and Traitor romance has seemingly made its way out of Alan’s Scottish castle.

During a joint postseason interview with Entertainment Weekly, Victor and Katie revealed they followed through on their plans and actually went on a first date.

“We have gone on that date. After incessant begging, let the kids back home know that persistence works,” Katie joked, before revealing that it was “great.”

“It was fun to get to know each other outside of her trying to murder me, me trying not to get murdered. It was nice to take a moment just for us,” Victor, who played as a Faithful while Katie was one of the season’s Traitors, told the outlet.

As for what their first date entailed? Well, there was certainly no discussion about their time on the show, since Victor banned any mention of it. And, honestly, who can blame him? After all, he was onto Katie’s strategy to potentially target him for murder.

While her crush on Victor helped her game, Katie admitted that she genuinely enjoyed hanging out with him and even grew close to him throughout the season. She also revealed that she didn’t immediately kill Victor off because she wasn’t aware of his suspicions toward her—though he had been side-eyeing her earlier on than she realized.

The pair remained coy about whether they’ll keep their showmance going offscreen. When asked if they had plans to go on a second date, Victor shared, “Let’s see. Listen, man, it’s gonna... I don’t know. Getting that first one was an uphill battle, but I think there could be.”

“I think there could be,” Katie added. “But we’ll see. Gonna have to keep persisting.”

#6802. Forget Stainless Steel Appliances: This Designer-Approved Style Is the New Kitchen Status Symbol

Turn your hardest-working appliance into a design moment.

The kitchen has evolved from a hardworking cook space into a multifunctional room for lounging, gathering, and entertaining. So naturally, appliances are changing, too: They need to be as beautiful as they are functional.

Many associate appliances with stainless steel—it has been the go-to choice in kitchens for decades. The material even took center stage in our 1939 Washable House, where it made classic worktables and countertops easier to clean. But today’s design is making room for more colorful choices.

The kitchen designers behind our 2026 Whole Home in Charleston embraced that shift, showing how the room’s most essential appliance—the range—can balance utility with style.

For the kitchen’s centerpiece, designer Susan Ford of Bennett Hofford chose a La Cornue CornuFé range in Quintessential Teal. White-veined marble, creamy cabinetry, and golden hardware frame the deep blue hue, which continues across the lower cabinets to anchor the design.

The color also plays beautifully against the plush terra-cotta stools lining the island. Designer Chloe Legras of Boxwood Avenue, who designed the adjacent great room, selected the stools and other kitchen accents.

Beyond its color, the range features millwork in mixed metals, giving the traditional detail a futuristic edge and adding character to the small but bespoke space. A Parisian-style range hood with gold details completes the look.

If you’re looking to elevate your kitchen, take a cue from our Whole Home. This design is bold enough to impress the design-obsessed and practical enough for the bustle of everyday life.

#6797. 35 Clever Tech Gifts That Will Upgrade Every Aspect of Their Day

Every item on this page was hand-picked by a House Beautiful editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

From smart bird feeders to mug warmers, there’s something for everyone.

As much as we love a new throw pillow or vase under the Christmas tree, we know that some of the most coveted presents are electronic. Cool tech gifts can genuinely change your giftee’s life for the better. They can help them travel better, upgrade their home, get them through the workday faster, or just make them smile. So whoever you’re shopping for, from the chronically online teen to the discerning coworker, will appreciate these genius gifts.

Just don’t forget to personalize the item to your recipient. If they have interests such as photography and videography, consider getting them a tripod or GoPro camera so they can document all their action-packed adventures. The nature lover in your life will appreciate a smart bird feeder, and the chef will use the smart stand mixer all the time.

Below, we rounded up dozens of the coolest tech gifts on the market. Of course, we included tested and approved favorites from our very own staff. The technology that changed our lives will make for some super thoughtful gifts.

The Brick duo is kind of the anti-tech tech gift. If your loved one has been wanting to cut down their screen time, this is the smartest tool to help them do that. It works as a separation between you and your most distracting apps. One that can’t be dismissed without physical interaction. “I keep a Brick at my desk and in my kitchen as barriers to my doomscrolling habit,” Associate Shopping Editor Janae McKenzie says. “But the Brick Night is a real game-changer—it’s Bluetooth location-based, so the moment I cross the threshold into my bedroom, all my social apps are locked, so I actually have to go to sleep at a decent hour.”

Poplights are playful, renter-friendly wall sconces that can fit in any space; no electrician required. Instead of hardwiring, they use a rechargeable battery pack, so you can display the warm light and vibrant color with no ugly wires in sight. Installation is as easy as attaching a few Command strips.

The steamer is the new wrinkle-releasing mainstay, leaving the iron in the past. But instead of merely spraying steam, the Nori Cloud Max comes with vacuum technology that pulls the clothes toward the steamer. This smooths out your clothes with way fewer passes.

This is the ultimate travel tech accessory for frequent fliers. “Any overpacker knows how valuable suitcase space is,” McKenzie says. “I bought two of these travel bags because of the genius mini-vacuum that comes with them. They work like space-saving bags, but small enough to fit in a carry-on and take on the go. No more calls to the hotel front desk for a vacuum.”

The easiest way to make your bathroom feel like a spa? Add a towel warmer. Stepping out of the shower, you can retrieve a fluffy towel from this heater to wrap up in, easing the harsh cold of tile floors. It comes with a timer, so you won’t have to worry about leaving it on overnight.

Yes, landlines are cool again. This retro receiver attachment plugs into your phone’s USB-C port, so when you pick up a call, you can have a more tactile experience. It comes with a mic and speaker, so it’s primed for everything from your next Zoom meeting to long-distance catch-up chats.

This bouncy LED lamp comes in three colors and two sizes. The spring-loaded body adds some whimsy to your home office. It lights up with a soft touch and provides up to eight hours of warm glow.

Canopy is known for its powerful water filtration, air purification, and humidifiers, but its latest launch is the most versatile. The inline shower filter is designed to work with any showerhead, from handheld extensions to rain showers. It’s compact and removes contaminants for cleaner skin and air.

Not everyone has the square footage for a full-scale smart fridge, so Rocco just launched a mini solution to that problem. The Mini Mini is sized for your desktop, countertop, or nightstand. Perfect for bedtime skin care essentials or a midday Diet Coke break.

We forget how stale the air quality gets. Birdie can help you remember. The mechanical indicator droops when you need to crack a window and let in some fresh air. It doesn’t hurt that the simple Danish design is so chic.

This speaker is a built-in party starter. It’s waterproof and drop-proof, so it’s perfect to bring to the beach, pool, or anywhere you need some powerful tunes. The steady bass and 28 hours of playtime keep up with any gathering. It’s even a built-in power bank.

No one knows they need an electric candle lighter until they have one. Take it camping, or just have a safer way to enjoy your candle collection. The slim, rose-gold profile is cute enough to leave on display.

We're not the first to call a desk pad "sexy," but something about this sleek Grove Made piece gives off that exact vibe. It's made from natural materials like pine rosin and wood flour, making it durable, antistatic, and fingerprint-resistant. The pad comes in a variety of eye-pleasing colors and five different sizes to elevate any office space.

You don't have to be an avid bird watcher to enjoy this smart feeder. Armed with a smart camera, the feeder can take photos of birds as they come to feast on the seeds, alert you with bird arrival notifications, and identify up to 1,000 species so you can learn about the types that drop by for a snack. You can attach Bird Buddy to a tree, pole, wall, or fence.

"Fellow's sleek Stagg EKG electric kettle is now undoubtedly the coolest thing in my kitchen," says tester Katelyn Lunders. "I used to put my stovetop kettle on while I was getting ready in the morning and then sprint to turn it off when it started screaming at me, but Fellow's kettle is free of an annoying whistle (and requires no cardio). Instead, it has a touch screen that allows you to pick your perfect temperature and then quickly brings the water to that point and holds it there until you're ready to pour it over your coffee or tea. A true revelation for hot beverage lovers everywhere."

"When I first met my husband's parents, I gifted his dad a digital picture frame so that he can keep up with our lives in NYC and travels (they live in Virginia)," says Senior Commerce Editor Marina Liao. "These types of frames are so convenient because you can upload photos directly from your phone, from anywhere in the world, and they will populate into the digital frame." The best part about Skylight is that should the Wi-Fi go down, this frame will still display the photos (you just can't receive anything until the connection is restored).

“I wanted a private proposal with just my now-husband and our dog, so he bought this tripod so we would be able to capture the moment,” says Liao. “It comes with a tiny remote, is easy to set up, and packs up well into a small bag. Though we don’t use the selfie ring light much, it is very bright and illuminating, so it’s perfect for content creators or if you take photos in poorly lit spots.”

A GoPro is the perfect gift for the adventure seeker in your life. Some features to note about this waterproof model: the hindsight captures 30 seconds before you hit record so you don't miss a moment, and it has a new and improved slow-mo capability, plus TimeWrap 3.0 and night lapse.

For the person who's always trying to repair or build electronics from scratch, they will love this all-encompassing tech toolkit. It includes a 64 precision bit driver set, tweezers, flex extension, opening tools, and an antistatic wristband housed in a polycarbonate case.

#6796. This St. Bart’s House Was Designed to Look Nothing Like Its Neighbors

Built for maximum family time, a converted boutique hotel puts the emphasis on entertaining, hanging out, and blending into the surroundings.

Few clients ask their designer to make the bedrooms in their vacation home smaller and simpler, but that was just the edit the homeowners made to plans for their house in St. Bart’s, says designer David Ries. “They didn’t want these humongous bedrooms with huge floating beds and massive bathrooms.” Instead, the couple wanted to be able to host as many people as possible, a request that didn’t surprise Ries, who’d already worked with the family on homes in Manhattan, Long Island, and Telluride. “It’s what this client has always asked for. They can’t have enough bedrooms.”

Situated on a steep hillside above one of St. Bart’s famously pristine white sand beaches, the house had initially been a boutique hotel. The plan was to turn it into a retreat for themselves and their adult children and grandchildren. The family are longtime visitors to the island—“the daughter told me it was her ‘happy place,’” says Ries, lead designer of the New York-based interior design firm Ries Hayes—and knew exactly what they wanted. Not the white box look popular with luxury new builds on the island, but instead something reminiscent of the “old St. Bart’s,” says East Hampton-based landscape designer Edwina von Gal, who, like Ries, has collaborated with the clients on other projects.

A Caribbean island is a challenging place to work under any circumstances. Everything has to be brought in from far away, including all the plants, which came by barge from Miami. St. Bart’s in particular requires anyone building to work with a local architect and engineer. In their initial proposal, this French team defaulted to that “slick and modernist” look, as von Gal describes it, with the oversized bedrooms and white color scheme that dominates much of the island’s luxury housing stock.

The design team pushed back—asking for a lot of bedrooms (eight) and generous living spaces. They also had strong opinions on the building materials. Given the island’s climate, the humid, salt-soaked air can be hard on everything. “If we did white paint, which is prominent throughout St. Bart's, it’s constant maintenance, constant painting,” says Ries. The design team insisted on cladding the buildings in Ipe, the durable tropical hardwood, which wears well. It mystified the local architects and set the complex apart from its neighbors. It also makes the buildings disappear into the greenery of the hillside.

But the architects quickly caught on to the new plans, and von Gal is eager to praise their technical accomplishments. Regulations require properties to conserve rainwater, so there are cisterns beneath the buildings, plus technology infrastructure and laundry facilities. “Working on the hill is so complex,” she says. “It's such a tight little package of technology and function. What they did was amazing.”

The team decided to reconfigure the property as a series of pavilions that face the view of the ocean and surrounding hills. The main entry is at the top of the steep hill, along with parking. The next level down is for family bedrooms; below that is a primary suite and study; and finally the fourth level is the main living space, including a playroom. “We made sure this playroom was front and center,” says Ries. “My client loves her grandkids.”

The playroom and study have built-in daybeds that can be used as spare guest rooms in a pinch. But the clients did get their eight bedrooms. “We know how to make compact bedrooms and make them very efficient, make them very livable, which allows us to fit in a lot of space for a lot of guests, and then that puts a lot of emphasis on having main entertaining spaces that are big and gracious,” says Ries. “The idea is that everybody hangs out together.”

Everything is united by generous outdoor spaces, which wind between the bedrooms. “Every room has an amazing view,” says Ries. “It feels almost like your own little treehouse in a way.” The bottom living area opens out to a pool and an expansive deck where the family eats most of their meals.

The house has an elevator, but stairs are the primary way the family moves between levels and finally down to the beach. “We hung on for a really great, gracious, fun set of stairs,” says von Gal. “They really introduce you to how amazing the different levels are and all the wonderful secret places.”

Durable fabrics and materials were chosen throughout and Ries made sure everything could be easily moved around and reconfigured. Outside on the main deck, three long dining tables from Sutherland were outfitted with casters so they could be maneuvered into a single long table or several smaller ones; 26 lightweight dining chairs can be pulled from different parts of the deck. If the infamous “Christmas winds” bring a downpour, the tables can even be moved indoors so a dinner party can proceed in inclement weather. Outdoor seating can be arranged as a 15-foot sofa or several love seats.

“It's so flexible,” says Ries. “We know this from working with a client that their needs for entertaining change every day, or sometimes a couple times a day.”

The kitchen is also efficient, designed to produce easy, clean meals. The family would rather spend time in the pool than over a stove. And no part of the living room is oriented around a TV. “They're readers,” says Ries. “There’s always stacks of new books when you go to their homes. So there’s all these cool little spots you can tuck into.”

Ultimately, the client and design team’s insistence on what they wanted paid off—but it wasn’t without a few moments of doubt. “When so many people are telling you ‘no, surely this is not what you want,’ it does start to erode your confidence a little,” says von Gal. “Then when it does come out well, there’s a real sense of triumph and satisfaction.”

Elizabeth Angell is a journalist and editor who writes about style, travel, parenting, and the British Royal Family. She has written for The New York Times and Town & Country and was most recently the Editor in Chief of Romper. She lives in Brooklyn, New York with her family.
